# Requirements register: requirement → test

Canonical map from every specification requirement to the test that will prove it, its
owning sprint and task, and its current status. Authority: product specification v1.3
(`Careerwright_Product_Specification.md`), then the local development plan v1.6
(`docs/plan/development-plan.md`), then `docs/plan/l0-sprint-tasks.md`. This register adds
test IDs and ownership only; it never relaxes a requirement. Requirement text is a short
paraphrase. Where a row and the specification differ, the specification wins and the row
is a defect to fix.

Created 2026-10-27 (Task 2, Sprint 0). Every task updates the rows it closes (implementer
contract, step 4).

**Plan v1.5 (2026-10-31, the product owner's approval of the audit).** Every open row whose
owner tasks were all complete is reassigned to a remaining task, or recorded as an L0
deferral (section 7). The rows for the new behaviour are added, each owned by its new task:
the role space (DIV1-13–15, PROF-10–12, MAP1-09, TEST1-15), the You chapter's finish
(STATE-09, STATE-10, UI-12), live sessions and the spending line (MODEL-10, MODEL-11), the
stored planning inputs (PLN-11), options for any target (OPTN-11, OPTN-12) and the facts
the route search lacks, supplied at the checkpoint (OPTN-13, from Task 13a's measurement).
The industry rows follow ADR 0014 (glyph and label), H-13 holds session 2's record, and the
sessions table holds the sessions as they now stand.

**Plan v1.6 (2026-11-01, the product owner's school-leaver decisions).** The rows for the new
behaviour are added, each owned by the task that builds it: no job yet (STATE-11, PROF-13,
PROF-14 and PROF-15, owned by T6f, with T10 and T12b reading the new answers), the synthetic
school leaver (TEST1-16, T6e), the tuition fee loan (FUNDING-06, T12c, shown by T12b and T14;
FUNDING-04 records the L0 change), the school-leaver option pack (OPTN-14, T7f), the
checkpoint for someone not working (OPTN-15, T12b), option research naming apprenticeships
and degrees (OPTN-16, T7e), a route longer than the model (PLN-12, T14), apprenticeship
listings (JOB-09, T8), the world answering a person with no job (WLD-12, TM) and the check of
the rules for young people's data (H-15, T16). The sessions table gains the school-leaver
pack session, and section 7 the known limit "free from September". No other row changes
here: each task updates the rows its work touches.

**Fidelity.** Every row was checked mechanically for structure: unique IDs, valid statuses,
and every spec ID and plan family present. On 2026-10-28 (Task 2, fix round 1) every row of
the FIN1, TIME1, OPT1, ROU1, RDY1, OUT1, VIS1, VIS2, AIR1 and DIV1 families was compared
against the specification text, and drift was corrected. The other rows have had the
structural check only. Treat their wording as a pointer to the spec section, not a
substitute for it.

## 1 How to use this register

**Test IDs** are `<AREA>-<NN>` and never reused or renumbered:

- `AREA` is a spec requirement ID without its hyphen (`RDY1-03` tests RDY-1), a plan
  section 8 regression family (`TRACE01-02`, `PAYCAP-01`), or a **register anchor** for an
  untagged M0 section (`STATE`, `PROF`, `ACQ`, `JOB`, `ASSESS`, `OPTN`, `FRESH`, `PLN`, `PLAN`,
  `EXP`, `MODEL`, `DUR`, `API`, `SEC`, `UI`), or `WLD` for the living world (plan §4, ADR 0014;
  added by Task W2E, Sprint W's SW.6). Anchors are this register's labels, not specification
  IDs.
- Tag each test with its ID so the register can be checked mechanically: the pytest marker
  `@pytest.mark.req("RDY1-03")` (registered in `backend/pyproject.toml` by Task 3; pytest
  runs with `--strict-markers`), and a `[RDY1-03]` prefix in Vitest and Playwright titles.
- Section 4 tables have no Evidence column: there the Status cell starts with the status
  and continues with its evidence. A shared row whose Task 3 part passed but whose other
  owners' parts are untested stays `planned` and says which part is done.
- One test may carry several IDs; one ID may need several tests. A row is closed only when
  every assertion it names is tested.

**Status** (the only allowed values):

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `planned` | No test yet. |
| `implemented` | A test exists but has not passed on the final candidate of its task. |
| `verified` | Passed on a named commit; the Evidence column says where. |
| `spike-verified` | Established by Sprint 0 spike code under `backend/spikes/`. The product module must re-establish it with its own test before the row becomes `verified`. |
| `blocked` | Cannot be tested now; the Evidence column gives the reason and owner. |
| `not run — requires human` | A human-only gate (ruling R2). Agents never mark these passed. |

**Layer:** D domain (pytest, Hypothesis) · A algorithm (independent oracle) · P persistence
(real PostgreSQL 16, DBOS) · AD adapter (recorded inputs, failure servers) · API (FastAPI
contract) · UI (Vitest, Testing Library, Playwright) · A11Y (axe, keyboard) · V visual
(screenshots) · M model quality (reviewed cases; live runs explicit and capped) · S security
and recovery · DOC document or ADR evidence · H human.

**Owners:**

| REC1-11 | A model-written "Why this could suit you" (D3, ADR 0008): at most one bounded model call per slate; it quotes only the person's confirmed answers and the grounded occupation record, each quotation verified server-side as a span of its source; validated like a search proposal (one repair, then `invalid_model_output`); no readiness or affordability claim; the deterministic provenance stays underneath, is shown in every case and is what exports cite; a failed explanation is shown as unavailable, never hidden | D, M, UI | T6b | verified (deterministic checks; model behaviour live-sampled once; accepted by the product owner on 2026-10-28, `docs/reviews/session-1.md` "Addendum": the readiness, pay and hiring block as answer 3 put it, the rule with its examples, and the automatic request, retry rule and placement; the full word lists, claim and quotation bounds, full function-word list and "first view" still provisional, H-12) | Task 6b (ADR 0007 decision 12): a `slate_explanation` run kind on the one activity, at most one current note per discovery (a partial unique index on the current link, migration 0012; a changed view is never a new slate, and asking again answers with the same run), over the slate's first view. Every quotation is verified as a span of the pinned answers or of that occupation's ESCO record and stored in the source's own characters; a misquotation, an invented answer, a fact ESCO lacks (another suggestion's record included), a claim without both quotations, quotation marks in a claim, a quotation without a content word ("and", "s work"), a readiness, affordability or hiring claim or phrasing that only the person's answers use, and an inferred characteristic are refused; past the count bounds nothing more is checked and the repair fits its reserved tokens; answers too long for the prompt in bytes are a typed refusal (`tests/domain/test_slate_explanation.py`, fix round 1); each gets one repair, then `invalid_model_output`, invalid JSON too, and a truncated output fails at once (`tests/domain/test_slate_explanation_agent.py`). End to end: one run per slate whatever the view, every quotation a span of its source read from the taxonomy API, the slate unchanged, the journal identifiers only and no leak; a failed or cut-off note is unavailable with its failure, nothing stored, the provenance kept, and a Try again refused (`SLATE_ALREADY_EXPLAINED`) once a request was sent; a note refused before any request (read from its attempts) may be tried again by an explicit click, the new run replacing the link, concurrent tries starting one run (fix round 1); refused before any run for a failed discovery or changed answers, or for answers none of which the check accepts quoted whole ("'odd jobs'", straight or curly; `test_an_answer_the_check_cannot_quote_whole_is_refused_before_any_run`, the pre-check and the demo explainer sharing `accepted_quotation`); the export (format 4) cites the ESCO URI and the discovery, never the note; a demo workspace uses the offline fixture explainer at no cost (`tests/integration/test_slate_explanations.py`); the submission's answer never holds the written text (receipts). UI: the note above "How Careerwright found it" in every state, saying only the quoted words were checked, pending, unavailable with the failure's or the refusal's words and its one action (Try again for a run that reached no model or a refused request, Suggest again otherwise), not covered after a view change, one automatic request per slate (`src/components/Directions.test.tsx`, `e2e/conversation.spec.ts`; stories `SlateOfSix`, `SlateNotePending`, `SlateNoteUnavailable`, `SlateNoteRetry`; captures `you--slate*` at three sizes). Offline evaluation EXP-01 over the full release (`tests/integration/test_evaluation.py`); live EXP-01 on the test model passed after one repair (ledger rows 25-26, `docs/evidence/live-eval.md`) |

### DIV-1 Diverse discovery beyond common careers (spec §4)

| DIV1-01 | A common-role template library is only a cache accelerator, never the set of recommendable careers; retrieval covers the full pinned English ESCO catalogue through PostgreSQL full-text search and alias lookup, with no embeddings, Career Causeways or extra taxonomy API | P | T5 | verified | Task 5, fix round 1: the full ESCO v1.2.1 release (3,039 occupations) is searched by PostgreSQL English full-text search (`match=all` or `any`) plus exact alias lookup that returns every occupation sharing a label, `tests/integration/test_taxonomy.py` (sample: aliases, hidden labels, shared labels, task words, totals, and a total of `null` when full-text search did not run: `test_a_label_match_with_no_searchable_words_reports_full_text_as_not_run`; full release: `test_the_full_pinned_release_imports_and_is_searchable`), and in the stack, `test_setup_imports_the_full_pinned_esco_release_into_the_stack`. No template library, embedding, Career Causeways or taxonomy API exists (ARC1-01 lock check). ADR 0006 |
| DIV1-02 | Several task, interest and skill queries; the combined pool is reranked; the bounded working pool is initially 60–120 distinct concepts where evidence permits (soft) | D, M | T6 | verified | Task 6: up to 8 model queries plus the current role and target titles, pooled, de-duplicated and capped at 120, filled round-robin across ISCO families before relevance cuts it, with the excess counted (`careerwright/profile/exploration.py`, `slate.py`; `test_slate.py::test_the_bounded_pool_samples_across_families_before_relevance_cuts_it`); `pool_size` and `beyond_pool_limit` are reported. Since Task W2B fix round 2 (ADR 0007 decision 13) a title is joined by shared essential skills and every other search reaches work only through one essential skill holding all its words, keeping every occupation it reaches up to the bound, ties ordered by the share of essential skills reached, never by title (`test_slate.py::test_equal_matches_go_to_the_larger_share_of_essential_skills_never_to_the_title`). Since fix round 3 no cut falls inside an exact tie: a join keeps its best 30 and every occupation tied with the 30th, and a search its bound and every occupation tied with the last (`test_skill_joins.py::test_a_join_never_cuts_inside_a_tie`, `tests/integration/test_evaluation.py::test_a_search_never_cuts_inside_a_tie`). A search that can find work reaches at most 50 occupations (decision 13's too-general rule), so it keeps all it reaches; `beyond_pool_limit` counts, once each, what a join reached past its cut and what the pool's bound left out (`test_slate.py::test_the_pool_counts_what_a_join_reached_past_its_cut_and_its_own_bound`, `test_skill_joins.py::test_a_join_says_how_many_it_reached_and_which_it_did_not_keep`), and the slate says what a join keeps ("... and keeps the 30 with the largest share of essential skills in common", since fix round 4, which named the share and not the count; since fix round 5 it says what Careerwright found: "Careerwright found 47 other occupations in ESCO that share 3 or more essential skills with “data analyst”"; `test_slate.py::test_the_pool_counts_what_a_join_reached_past_its_cut_and_its_own_bound`, `Directions.test.tsx`). On the full release (`tests/integration/test_evaluation.py`): four searches reaching 178 occupations fill the pool to 120 with 58 counted (`test_the_working_pool_keeps_its_bound_and_counts_the_work_beyond_it`), and a search reaching 44 occupations through one skill keeps them all (`test_a_search_keeps_all_the_work_one_skill_reaches_so_no_tie_is_cut_by_title`). The 60 is soft: P02's evidence reaches 12 occupations, and a full slate below 60 says how many it chose from while a short one says why it is short (DSC-01, DSC-07, DSC-08, DSC-10, DSC-19 `small_pool_has_reason`; DSC-06, DSC-07, DSC-08, DSC-12, DSC-16, DSC-17, DSC-18, DSC-19 `fewer_has_reason`) |

The work-environment channel's source is how the person likes to work: Task W2B fix round 2 stopped every search resting on it alone, which left the channel without a source; since fix round 3 only a one-word answer ("structured") is not searched, and a longer one is searched like any other answer (provisional, for session W2): "working outdoors" reaches 12 occupations through "work in outdoor conditions" (`tests/integration/test_evaluation.py::test_where_someone_likes_to_work_is_searched_and_a_specific_answer_s_work_comes_first`). Not done: oversampling under-retrieved catalogue areas and reviewed transition datasets (M1, Career Causeways) |

The live check of the deeper search is the controller's. Round 2 (the product owner's live try, 3 November 2026; ADR 0009 round 2 line, migration 0048): the candidates are Muse's picks, then every other page the search returned, up to 40 (`tests/domain/test_role_research.py::test_muse_s_picks_come_first_then_every_other_page_the_search_returned`, `::test_at_most_forty_pages_are_kept_to_try`); with no page returned nothing is read (`::test_when_the_search_tool_returns_no_page_nothing_is_read`); a page of search results for the title is never read (`::test_a_search_results_page_for_the_title_is_never_read`); `role-search-3` asks for at least four searches; a page no longer there (404 or 410) is never a page without the title and the reading goes on to the next, until 20 are read (`tests/integration/test_role_research.py::test_a_page_no_longer_there_is_never_a_page_without_the_title_and_the_reading_goes_on`, `::test_a_reading_reads_at_most_twenty_pages_and_tries_no_more_once_it_has`, passed under the lock; the live tool, `tests/tools/test_live_role_research.py::test_the_tool_reads_as_the_product_does_past_pages_gone_until_twenty_are_read`; the web's "No longer there", `RoleResearch.test.tsx`); each research states `sources_returned`, `candidates`, `pages_read` and `pages_gone`. |

### AIR-1 AI Resilience Score (spec §5)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AIR1-01 | Labelled "AI Resilience Score", 0–10, higher is more resilient, five-year outlook, led by a range. M0 publishes the range only, marked experimental, with inherited geography and no unsupported seniority or local modifiers; the reference-score mode is M1. The range is sensitivity to scenarios and mapping, not a confidence interval. Synthetic scores are conspicuously labelled | UI | T8 | planned | |
| AIR1-02 | Only reviewed range records publish: a reliable mapping, explicit technological and geographic scope, supporting evidence for the band, recorded contradictions and at least two independent evidence families; the reviewer's decision record holds scope, assumptions, method version, reasons and any widening; otherwise the assessment is null. The application performs matching, publication-state checks and filtering deterministically | D | T7 | verified | Task 7 slice B (ADR 0010 decision 7): `ai_resilience.publication_failures` checks each condition deterministically (reviewer decision, reviewed mapping, both scopes, observed supporting evidence, two families from two independent sources, contradictions recorded, physical automation stated, band edges or a documented anchor, five-year horizon, method version), and a record failing any is never published; a reviewed null decision is unscored with its reason, `test_ai_resilience.py::test_a_reviewed_complete_record_publishes`, `test_a_record_missing_any_publication_condition_never_publishes` (18 cases in the slice, 24 after fix round 1), `test_a_reviewed_null_assessment_is_unscored_with_its_reason`; the product pack holds no record (no accountable reviewer, H-10: every real role unscored) and the one labelled synthetic record publishes only in the fixture workspace, `test_the_committed_packs_hold_no_real_record_and_one_labelled_synthetic_one`, `test_reference_data.py::test_the_synthetic_fixture_record_publishes_only_in_the_fixture_workspace`. Fix round 1 (review B I1, M1, M2, M6): exposure evidence alone (task exposure, observed use) fails `exposure_only_evidence`, so the fixture now also carries a labelled synthetic accountability family bearing protection; the decision records the bounds it approved and a record whose bounds differ fails `bounds_not_decided`; the evidence quality is required, and a range across bands needs a widening reason; a reviewed null skips the evidence-sufficiency checks and reads `reviewed_null`, `test_a_record_missing_any_publication_condition_never_publishes` (24 cases), `test_a_reviewed_null_for_thin_evidence_reports_the_reviewer_s_decision` |
| AIR1-03 | No automatic formula (for example 10 − exposure, a product of indices, or absence of AI usage read as protection) exists in code; where evidence supports exposure only, the exposure is shown and resilience stays unscored | D | T7 | verified | Task 7 slice B (ADR 0010 decisions 6, 7): the assessment reads only a pack of reviewed records, and the record module imports no evidence table, so no formula over exposure, observed use or their absence exists; an occupation with ILO exposure and observed use shows both (`ai_evidence.state: exposure_only`) and resilience `unscored`, `no_record`, `test_ai_resilience.py::test_no_score_is_derived_from_evidence_in_code`, `test_reference_data.py::test_exposure_and_observed_use_are_shown_and_resilience_stays_unscored`; a missing observation offered as protection fails `missing_use_as_protection`. Fix round 1 (review B I1): the publication checks themselves now keep exposure-only evidence unscored (`exposure_only_evidence`), not only the absence of real records; and beside a published range the imported evidence reads `assessed`, never `exposure_only`, `test_a_record_missing_any_publication_condition_never_publishes`, `test_reference_data.py::test_imported_exposure_is_never_called_exposure_only_beside_a_published_range` |

### RDY-1 Readiness and apply-now (spec §10)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RDY1-01 | Ready iff every essential skill row's evidenced level ≥ its required level and all credential, experience and eligibility gates are satisfied, for a fixed target/matrix version; unknown does not pass | D | T9 | verified | Task 9 final candidate (ADR 0012 decision 7): ready only with every required skill row at its required evidenced level and every required credential, experience and eligibility row satisfied (protected gates verified); no evidence is `no_usable_evidence`, never level 0, `test_capability_readiness.py::test_readiness_needs_every_required_row_and_gate_and_unknown_never_passes`. Required means essential, disputed (provisional) or a protected gate. Fix round 2 (N5, N6, provisional): a required row no answer can settle beside modelled ones is `insufficient_coverage`, and the right to work alone is not coverage, `test_capability_readiness.py::test_a_requirement_answers_cannot_settle_is_a_coverage_gap_beside_modelled_rows`, `::test_an_eligibility_gate_alone_is_not_coverage`. Fix round 3 (R2-1): a known shortfall stays `not_ready` beside a coverage gap, which is carried as its own blocker, `::test_a_known_shortfall_stays_not_ready_beside_a_requirement_no_answer_can_settle` |

### TIME-1 Monthly decision contract (spec §12)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TIME1-01 | A month is a named calendar month in the work area's timezone; actual start, finish, application and payment dates are kept on cards and exports and shown beside model months | D, UI | T11, T14, T15 | planned | T11 part verified (Task 11 final candidate; ADR 0013 decision 2): months are named calendar months (`tests/domain/test_planning_months.py`), and each compiled variant keeps its actual start and finish beside its model months (`test_planning_trace.py::test_actual_dates_stay_beside_the_model_months`); the pack adapter says when an intake is a month only. Open: cards and exports, T14 and T15 |
| TIME1-02 | Every touched month reserved; a prerequisite is usable only from the next month boundary after completion; no snapping earlier or rounding down | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: TIME-01, TIME-02, TIME-04 and TRACE01-08 (`test_planning_months.py`, Hypothesis over dates; `test_planning_checks.py::test_a_prerequisite_is_usable_only_from_the_month_after_its_completion`) |
| TIME1-03 | An application completes in a month strictly before the deadline month unless a completed application or admission is verified in the initial state; otherwise `COARSE_MODEL_UNRESOLVED` ("not resolved by this monthly model"), never "ineligible"; a source deadline still cannot be ignored after the date passes | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: TIME-03 (`test_planning_checks.py::test_an_application_in_its_deadline_month_is_unresolved_never_ineligible`, `::test_a_deadline_that_has_passed_cannot_be_ignored`; Hypothesis over the month rule) |
| TIME1-04 | Workload envelope, evaluated once per active month with no weekly start-decision variables: Σ maximum weekly loads ≤ minimum weekly availability, counting employment, study, commute and fixed care commitments consistently; total hours without a timetable leave the envelope unknown until an assumption is confirmed; a known session clash fails separately | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: WORKLOAD-01 to WORKLOAD-04 and TRACE01-07 (`test_planning_checks.py`, `test_planning_trace.py`) |
| TIME1-05 | Conservative prefunding each month: opening cash + receipts proven available before the outflows − outflows (living costs, transition payments, debt service) ≥ reserve; other receipts roll to the next month; no borrowing against an unaccepted offer or unconfirmed grant; a conservative rejection is flagged for review, never "cannot afford" | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: PREFUND-01 to PREFUND-03 and TRACE01-02; the ledger identities by Hypothesis (`test_planning_properties.py`); a rejection is `not_feasible_in_this_model` |
| TIME1-06 | One common comparison horizon covering the whole bounded planning horizon of the comparison; costs or lost earnings deferred beyond the window never manufacture an advantage; income after readiness follows a stated continuation scenario; nominal integer GBP minor units; user-confirmed net incomes; no tax or benefit inference from gross pay | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: ECON-01, ECON-04, ECON-05; one `PlanningInputs` per comparison; an advertised gross wage is information, only a confirmed take-home `Wage` is income (`test_planning_pack.py::test_an_apprenticeship_is_one_composite_action_with_its_wage_hours_and_training`) |

### FIN-1 Objective definitions (spec §12)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FIN1-01 | Default objectives: months to the modelled application-readiness milestone (required evidence, credentials, experience and waits; not an employment guarantee) and economic cost against a confirmed baseline over a common horizon; time to a target job is a separate conditional result | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate (ADR 0013 decision 8): the milestone is the capability model's, asked at each month boundary (`test_planning_trace.py::test_route_b_is_the_print_snapshot_s_route`, `test_planning_checks.py::test_delay_and_no_offer_change_both_the_time_and_the_income`); economic cost against the confirmed baseline; time to a target job is not computed |
| FIN1-02 | Direct cost covers tuition, assessment, materials, necessary equipment, additional travel or childcare, relocation and financing charges, less confirmed applicable non-repayable support; contractual liabilities extending past the horizon are included with their timing disclosed; loan principal is a liquidity source, not a discount or an additional copy of tuition cost | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: ECON-04, FUNDING-01, FUNDING-03; a loan is never support (FUNDING-04); a payment due before the planning start is listed and named, never dropped, and leaves the direct cost unknown (fix round 1, `test_planning_checks.py::test_a_payment_due_before_the_planning_start_is_named_never_dropped`) |
| FIN1-03 | Economic cost = direct + Σ (baseline − route income); wages counted once | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: TRACE01-01, ECON-01, ECON-02; the identity by Hypothesis (`test_planning_properties.py`) |
| FIN1-04 | Offer earnings start only at the offer gate; delay and no-offer change both time and income; unaccepted offers and possible funding are labelled conditions | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: FUNDING-02, PREFUND-03, `test_planning_checks.py::test_delay_and_no_offer_change_both_the_time_and_the_income`; conditions carry `confirm_offer_accepted` or `verify_funding_award` |
| FIN1-05 | Negative economic cost supported (`SignedMoney`) and never shown as cash available today; no fee-churning or artificial job switches | D, UI | T11, T14 | planned | T11 part verified (Task 11 final candidate): ECON-03. Open: the display, T14; fee-churning is excluded by one start per concept and exclusive groups (PLN-03), the search's own guard is T13a |
| FIN1-06 | Direct-spend and monthly-payment caps are hard; an economic-cost cap is optional and distinct; a deficit cap is separately named | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: PAYCAP-01 to PAYCAP-03 and `test_planning_checks.py::test_the_direct_budget_payment_cap_deficit_cap_and_reserve_are_four_limits` (the economic-cost cap is a preference); an unstated direct-spend cap, monthly payment cap or reserve is unresolved, never a pass (fix round 1, `::test_an_unknown_hard_limit_is_unresolved_never_a_pass`); the deficit cap's definition is provisional (R6, ADR 0013 decision 6; session 2 D14) |
| FIN1-07 | A missing baseline gives an unresolved economic comparison; "Compare direct spending only" is an explicit mode whose wording appears on charts and dominance explanations; only economic mode addresses lost earnings; never a silent switch (P02) | D, UI | T11, T14 | planned | T11 part verified (Task 11 final candidate): ECON-06 (`baseline_missing`, no economic value; the explicit `direct_spending` basis is carried on every result). Open: the wording on charts, T14 |
| FIN1-08 | Direct expenditure, income change, peak payments, cash reserve and debt always shown separately | UI | T14, T15 | planned | |
| FIN1-09 | Favourable, base and adverse scenarios carry no probabilities unless empirically calibrated; p is scenario-wise dominant over q only if it is no worse on time and cost in every declared scenario, with at least one strict comparison; base-case dominance alone is labelled "better in the base scenario", with reversals or unresolved scenarios marked; worst-case-vector dominance is not dominance in every paired scenario; no invented percentile labels | D | T11, T14 | planned | T11 part verified (Task 11 final candidate): every route is evaluated under the same four fixed scenarios, with no probability (`evaluate_route`; PLN-08). Open: scenario-wise dominance and its labels, T13a and T14 |
| FIN1-10 | Liquid-balance ledger with take-home income, cash grants, loan draws, committed living outgoings, direct payments and loan repayments; balance ≥ reserve; payments + repayments ≤ monthly cap; provider-paid support counted on both sides or neither; the debt balance is tracked separately, with interest accrual and principal repayment | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: FUNDING-03, FUNDING-04 (the debt ledger with integer interest), PREFUND family, the identities by Hypothesis; payments plus repayments within the monthly cap, existing debt included (`P_m + R_m ≤ M_m`, fix round 1, `test_planning_checks.py::test_existing_debt_repayments_count_inside_the_monthly_payment_cap`). M0 plans no loan draw. Whether existing repayments should leave the cap is session 2's D15 (R6) |
| FIN1-11 | The baseline is a user-confirmed counterfactual (staying in the current role, continuing a job search or another), never assumed to be staying put | D | T11 | verified | Task 11 final candidate: `Baseline` is a labelled, cited input and never defaulted; ECON-06 (P02's income of 0 is not a baseline) |
| FIN1-12 | Every route in a panel uses the same start, horizon, currency, baseline and scenario definitions; no earnings window ends at a route's own completion; immediate target employment is never inferred; amounts are nominal by default, and any inflation or discounting mode states its common assumptions | D | T11, T14 | planned | T11 part verified (Task 11 final candidate): one `PlanningInputs` per comparison, nominal, with the continuation scenario after each route (ECON-01, ECON-05). |---|---|---|---|---|---|

| ROU1-02 | A family keeps its meaningful efficient schedules, grouped under one family card with dates as expandable variants; no single best schedule or fixed cost per structure is assumed | D | T13a | verified | Task 13a final candidate: every schedule of a family is kept with its own dates, cost and layer, `test_route_search.py::test_a_later_intake_is_a_schedule_of_the_same_family_and_the_family_shows_its_best_layer`; see FAMILY |

Open: the label on screen (T14) |
| ROU1-04 | Overview shows distinct families first; suppresses repeat cards, never evidence | UI | T14 | planned (backend; screens in 14B. Task 14A candidate: families best layer first with their best schedule and counts; every schedule in the pages, `test_route_comparison.py`) | |

### OPT-1 Solver architecture (spec §12)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPT1-01 | Deterministic exhaustive DFS over the reviewed finite monthly catalogue in canonical action order: per concept, skip or one reviewed start; mutually exclusive variants, prerequisites, capacity, cash and target gates enforced; each allowed selected offering set visited exactly once; order and parallelism come from dates and the action DAG, never from permuting lists | A | T13a | verified | Task 13a final candidate (ADR 0016 decision 2; `planning/search.py`): canonical order, skip or one start per concept, at most six actions, one per exclusive group and one bridge role, every allowed set visited once and checked by `check_route`, `test_route_search.py::test_each_allowed_set_is_visited_once_and_never_more_than_six_actions`, `::test_one_start_per_concept_one_per_exclusive_group_and_one_bridge_role`, `::test_parallel_prerequisites_are_kept_parallel_and_before_the_course_they_open`; agreement with the oracle, `test_route_oracle.py`. Ruling: one bridge role is one `work` action |
| OPT1-02 | Prune only irreparable violations or certified bounds under the actual model, each documented with its proof; never on first-front dominance, dominator count, current readiness or accumulated economic cost; lower bounds never ignore future funding; stop-at-readiness pruning only under a proved rule that puts every extension outside the route scope | A, DOC | T13a | verified | Task 13a final candidate: six rules, each proved in `planning/search.py`'s docstring and ADR 0016 decision 4 (input-level unknowns; a variant's own findings; spend caps by additivity; work-pattern conflicts and clashes; workload unless a later work pattern can lower the job's hours; a prerequisite no later variant grants in time); never the reserve, deficit cap, applications, readiness or objectives. Each rule cuts only what it proves, `test_route_search.py::test_a_spend_cap_prunes_only_sets_that_exceed_it`, `::test_a_monthly_payment_cap_prunes_only_months_that_exceed_it`, `::test_a_workload_breach_prunes_only_when_no_later_option_can_lower_the_job_s_hours`, `::test_a_workload_breach_no_later_option_can_relieve_is_pruned`, `::test_a_prerequisite_a_later_concept_can_grant_in_time_is_never_pruned`, `::test_a_superset_that_lowers_the_economic_cost_is_kept_and_ranks_first`; soundness against the oracle, `test_route_oracle.py::test_the_search_finds_exactly_the_oracle_s_feasible_schedules_objectives_and_layers` (Hypothesis; also run on 4,000 examples) |
| OPT1-03 | Every distinct feasible schedule encountered, dominated ones included, is persisted to a versioned disk-backed archive with atomic checkpoints and an idempotent solve identity; recovery resumes a proven checkpoint or restarts under a new attempt ID | P | T13b | planned | L0 deferral (spec section 22, the product owner's yes, 2026-10-31, on T13a's measured sizes): no disk-backed archive, checkpoints or resume. T13b keeps every distinct feasible schedule encountered, dominated ones included, in memory under the caps, with an idempotent solve identity; a crashed solve is a failed run, started again under a new attempt ID, never a truncated result. T13b part verified (Task 13b final candidate; ADR 0016 decisions 14 to 16): every distinct feasible schedule met, dominated ones included, is kept in memory under the caps and stored with the ranked result; a state-limited run's archive is the complete run's up to where it stopped, `test_route_search.py::test_a_state_limited_run_replays_exactly_and_keeps_every_schedule_it_met`; the solve identity (`solve_id`) joins the same solve while it is under way, `tests/integration/test_route_solves.py::test_asking_twice_is_one_solve_and_what_a_solve_cannot_start_from_is_refused`, `test_route_solve_records.py::test_one_snapshot_under_the_same_limits_is_one_solve`; a worker killed mid-solve leaves its attempt `interrupted` and the solve starts again under a new attempt, whose result alone is stored, `test_route_solves.py::test_a_crashed_solve_starts_again_under_a_new_attempt_never_a_truncated_result`. Task 13b fix round 1: a result stored before a crash is published and never solved again, `::test_a_result_stored_before_a_crash_is_published_and_never_solved_again`; a solve whose worker keeps crashing fails `unknown_failure` after three attempts, each `interrupted`, `::test_a_solve_whose_worker_keeps_crashing_fails_after_three_attempts`; a request after a cancel is a new run, never the cancelled one, `::test_a_solve_asked_for_again_after_its_cancel_is_a_new_run`. Stays planned for the deferred disk archive (section 7) |
| OPT1-04 | Caps: 30 s, 1,000,000 expanded states, 50,000 archived feasible schedules per target; archive persistence counts in the budget; exact stop reason recorded | A, P | T13b | verified | Task 13b final candidate: the M0 limits are the run's (recorded on `route_solves`, read before each candidate), each stop names its reason (LIMITS-01 to LIMITS-03); with no disk archive (L0 deferral, section 7) nothing is persisted inside the search, and the stored result's facts repeat its stop reason under CHECKs, `test_route_solves.py::test_a_search_its_limit_stops_is_stored_truncated_never_no_route`, `::test_the_database_repeats_the_search_s_rules`. Task 13b fix round 1 (ADR 0016 decisions 19 and 22): solves run one at a time in the worker, so a search's 30 seconds are its own (another waits queued, its clocks starting when it runs), `test_route_solves.py::test_two_solves_asked_together_run_one_at_a_time_each_on_its_own_clock`; the cancel is read on a thread of its own, so a slow store is never charged to the search's clock or the rank stage's, `test_route_solve_runs.py::test_a_slow_store_is_never_charged_to_the_search_s_clock` |
| OPT1-05 | `search_coverage` is `complete` only after exhausting the declared tree with sound pruning, else `budget_truncated`; operational states queued, running, succeeded, failed, cancelled, invalid-input stay distinct; a crash is failed | P | T13a (coverage), T13b (run states) | verified | T13b part verified (Task 13b final candidate): a solve is a run, queued, running, then complete, failed or cancelled; invalid input is refused with its code and no run, `test_route_solves.py::test_a_solve_missing_what_it_needs_is_refused_naming_it_never_run`, `::test_asking_twice_is_one_solve_and_what_a_solve_cannot_start_from_is_refused`; a cancelled or failed solve has no result, `::test_a_cancel_reaches_the_search_at_its_next_check_and_stores_nothing`, `::test_a_rank_stage_past_its_own_limit_fails_the_run_and_claims_no_ranks`; a crashed attempt is `interrupted`, never a truncated result, `::test_a_crashed_solve_starts_again_under_a_new_attempt_never_a_truncated_result`; the database refuses a stopped search stored as complete, `::test_the_database_repeats_the_search_s_rules`. T13a part verified (Task 13a final candidate): `complete` only when the tree is exhausted, else `budget_truncated` with its stop reason (state, wall time or archive limit), what it found kept, `test_route_search.py::test_trace_01_completes_with_a_b_c_in_f1_d_in_f2_and_e_in_f3`, `::test_a_run_stopped_at_its_state_limit_is_budget_truncated_with_its_reason`, `::test_a_run_stopped_by_the_clock_says_so`, `::test_a_full_archive_stops_the_run_before_a_schedule_goes_unrecorded`; an input beyond the M0 bounds is refused, never searched in part, `::test_a_catalogue_beyond_the_m0_bounds_is_refused_never_searched_in_part`. ### PROF Structured profile and conversational integrity (spec §4)

| ID | Requirement | Layer | Owner | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PROF-01 | Every field has value, epistemic status, source reference, observation date, verification state and provenance; "no preference" ≠ "not yet asked" | D | T5 | verified (Task 5 final candidate ef25e31): each field has a state (not asked, answered, unknown, skipped, prefer not to say) and a value only when answered; each answer cites a source reference with its kind (direct entry, fixture, conversation), observation time and recorder; a conversation source adds the proposing turn, the cited messages, the basis (stated or inferred) and the person who confirmed it, `tests/integration/test_interview_turns.py::test_hard_constraints_apply_only_when_each_is_confirmed`, `tests/domain/test_profile_document.py`. Fix round 1: the basis is the server's, not the model's word: "stated" only when every new value appears in the cited messages, otherwise "inferred" (`tests/domain/test_interview_contract.py::test_stated_is_kept_only_when_the_cited_messages_contain_the_value` and the three tests after it; a no-value answer shown as inferred is provisional, H-11). Direct entries are the person's own statements; their basis is their kind (ADR 0006 decision 6) |
| PROF-02 | Native structured output then server-side validation; never a silent switch to prose parsing | AD | T5 | verified (Task 5 final candidate ef25e31): the interview agent asks for `NativeOutput` (strict JSON schema, no strict-mode-breaking keywords) and every output passes `check_turn` server-side; there is no prose-parsing path, and a refusal or invalid output fails typed, `tests/domain/test_interview_contract.py`, `tests/domain/test_interview_agent.py` |
| PROF-03 | Changes commit only after complete validation; partial streamed JSON never commits | D, P | T5 | verified (Task 5, fix round 1): a run output exists only after complete validation; a response cut off at its token limit ends `output_truncated` at once, unparsed, with nothing stored; the streamed reply lives only in the sealed partial store, is superseded by the validated output or marked incomplete on failure, and never reaches the profile, which changes only through a decision on a complete proposal (ADR 0006 decision 13), `tests/integration/test_interview_turns.py::test_a_refusal_or_a_truncated_output_ends_typed_with_the_profile_intact`, `test_a_reply_cut_off_mid_stream_ends_observably_with_the_partial_marked_incomplete`, `test_a_turn_streams_its_reply_to_its_owner_and_the_validated_output_supersedes_it`, `tests/domain/test_interview_agent.py::test_a_truncated_output_fails_at_once_and_is_never_parsed_or_repaired` |

`tests/domain/test_fetcher.py` against a controlled loopback server: names resolved at connect time and refused if any answer is private, the connection made to the checked address, every redirect re-checked (private and encoded targets, another port, a name whose answer is private), at most 5 hops (ADR 0009 decision 3); only checked http(s) URLs in URL fields (fix round 1): a refused redirect target is never recorded, `test_fetcher.py::test_a_redirect_to_a_private_or_encoded_address_is_blocked_and_never_recorded`, https never redirects to http, `test_https_never_redirects_down_to_http`, a refused submission is its refusal and host as text with no URL, `test_acquisition.py::test_a_run_whose_every_source_is_refused_is_source_blocked_and_records_no_unsafe_url`, a pasted source is a checked URL or refused, `test_a_pasted_text_s_source_is_kept_only_as_a_checked_url`, and earlier rows cleaned and CHECKs added by migration 0014, `test_migrations.py::test_0014_leaves_only_http_urls_in_url_fields`; fix round 2: a search proposal sealed earlier with a raw URL is re-checked whenever read, `test_source_search.py::test_a_proposal_sealed_with_a_raw_url_is_never_served_with_it`, and any non-ASCII host character whose NFKC form holds a digit or "." is refused (U+2024, U+FE52), `test_addresses.py::test_encoded_address_forms_are_refused`; proxy mode for the controller's tools only (ADR 0009 amendment, 2026-11-01): CONNECT through an explicit proxy with no name looked up and TLS verified against the named bundle, every URL and redirect check, robots.txt, the size limit and a proxy's refusal (never a site's status) held, `tests/domain/test_fetch_proxy.py` against a local fake proxy; the restricted worker refuses it (`test_the_restricted_worker_s_settings_refuse_proxy_mode`, `test_the_restricted_worker_builds_its_fetcher_without_a_proxy`), the fetcher ignores proxy variables in the environment, and no product module names it (`test_no_product_module_can_reach_proxy_mode`); a body whose normalised visible text still shows a detail (a no-break-space entity, a number split by newlines, a zero-width space, a soft hyphen) is not kept, `body_not_kept: visible_text_detail` (migration 0022), `test_a_kept_body_that_shows_a_detail_once_normalised_is_not_kept`; a script hiding one behind a JavaScript escape (`\xNN`, `\u{...}`, octal; probe case 15) is `block_unredactable`, `test_a_script_hiding_a_detail_behind_a_javascript_escape_means_no_body`; the model's input is normalised, then redacted, then cut, `tests/domain/test_extraction.py::test_the_model_input_is_normalised_before_it_is_redacted`; fix round 4 (re-review 4 A R4-1, item 1): in normalised text an email with whitespace around its "@" or its domain's dots (the TLD letters only) is found and removed, so an email split by inline markup (`jane<span>@</span>recruit.example`, `jane@<b>recruit</b>.example`) means a kept body is not kept (`visible_text_detail`), `tests/domain/test_redaction.py::test_a_kept_body_with_an_email_split_by_inline_markup_is_not_kept`, and leaves the model's input, `tests/domain/test_extraction.py::test_the_model_input_holds_no_email_split_by_inline_markup`, while a sentence with " @ " and no domain is left unchanged, `test_redaction.py::test_an_ordinary_sentence_with_an_at_sign_and_no_domain_keeps_its_body_unchanged`, `test_extraction.py::test_the_model_input_keeps_an_ordinary_sentence_with_an_at_sign_and_no_domain`; an attribute's value is decoded and looked at, so an entity-encoded `mailto:` link with no visible address means no body (`attribute_detail`, migration 0025), `test_redaction.py::test_an_entity_encoded_mailto_link_with_no_visible_address_means_no_body`, and a plain one loses its address in place, `test_a_plain_mailto_link_loses_its_address_and_the_body_is_kept`; fix round 5 (re-review 5 A R5-1, R5-2): a sentence starting with a capital after "@" and a full stop is no address, so "Follow @GOVUK. Sign up" and "Drop in @ 10am. Bring photo ID." keep their body and stay in the model's input, `test_redaction.py::test_an_ordinary_sentence_with_an_at_sign_and_no_domain_keeps_its_body_unchanged`, `test_extraction.py::test_the_model_input_keeps_an_ordinary_sentence_with_an_at_sign_and_no_domain`, R4-1's forms still caught (above); a percent-encoded `mailto:` in an `href` means no body (`attribute_detail`), `test_redaction.py::test_a_percent_encoded_mailto_link_with_no_visible_address_means_no_body`; a JSON-LD link to an address (plain, entity- or percent-encoded) means no body (`link_detail`, migration 0026), `test_a_json_ld_link_to_an_address_means_no_body`; an entity-encoded email in a JSON-LD string leaves the body and the description claim's excerpt, `test_an_entity_encoded_email_in_a_json_ld_string_leaves_the_body_and_its_excerpt`; an apply link holding an address, as written or decoded, is no claim on every path, `test_pages.py::test_an_apply_link_holding_a_third_party_s_detail_is_no_claim`, `test_adzuna.py::test_an_adzuna_link_holding_a_third_party_s_detail_is_no_claim`, `test_extraction.py::test_a_quoted_apply_link_that_holds_an_address_once_decoded_is_dropped`; Task 7 slice B (R6-1): an image file extension is never an address's TLD, so `logo@2x.png` in a JSON-LD `logo`, a percent-encoded `srcset`, a plain `src` or `srcset` keeps the body and the path, `test_redaction.py::test_an_image_named_at_2x_keeps_the_body_and_its_path`, `test_an_image_file_name_is_never_read_as_an_email_address`, and real addresses at real TLDs are still removed, `test_real_addresses_are_still_removed_beside_the_image_rule`; a URL carrying a credential refused before anything is recorded; real attempt cases X01 (404) and X02 (LinkedIn, `source_denied`) in `corpus/manifest.json` |

0.1.0 keeps its pin. Task 7b part done, 2026-10-29). The format is `careerwright.catalogue.option_pack` (ADR 0011): Pydantic models, and a validator that checks every value is cited, its claim's span, review records, outcome levels and splits. Every value has the one shape its field declares (money as integer GBP minor units, pay, ISO dates, deadlines, durations, schedules, instalments and the rest), and each record field checks the claims it cites against its own shape (ADR 0011 decision 13). The JSON Schema export states those shapes and is committed beside the packs (`england-option-pack.schema.json`, checked against the export by `tests/tools/test_option_pack_files.py`). Pack 0.1.0 is committed at `backend/src/careerwright/catalogue/packs/england_option_pack/`: only excerpts of at most 200 characters and no page body, 212 KB, written in the format's canonical form by `tools.option_pack_files --pack` and pinned by SHA-256 in `PINS`, so a changed byte does not load. Re-cut in fix rounds 1 and 2 before any push or consumer, and frozen from the end of Task 7b. It holds 33 sources, 481 claims, 20 job postings, 60 skill requirement rows, 10 options and offerings, 18 outcomes and 3 funding instruments, every record `proposed` and dated. The fast checks load and validate it (`tests/domain/test_option_pack.py`). The reviewed version is 0.2.0 (ADR 0011 decision 15). Task 7f part verified (2026-11-01): 0.3.0 adds the school-leaver sources, every new record `proposed`; 0.4.0, pinned `d005713b404bcbe911ee9c2d4e7d0c9f9117013e059159d75a82487fcc01b3c8`, publishes the school-leaver pack session's review of it the same way (`tests/tools/test_school_leaver_review.py`); an offering may name a further page it cites (`Offering.source_ids`, optional, so every earlier version writes byte for byte as before; `tests/domain/test_option_pack.py`); 0.1.0, 0.2.0 and 0.3.0 keep their pins (ADR 0011 amendments of 2026-11-01)) |

an acquisition cancelled mid-fetch hands no page over (`acquisition_cancelled`), `test_an_acquisition_cancelled_while_fetching_hands_no_page_to_extraction`; an extraction cancelled while its call is out keeps its result and its page reads as what was recorded, `test_an_extraction_cancelled_while_its_call_is_out_keeps_and_shows_its_result`; fix round 4 (review C-1 N1, N2): a recording whose page is model-readable takes the cancellation lock first, so a cancel concurrent with recording a kept-body page ends without a store error on either side (two connections: one holds the workspace's row while the cancel waits there holding its lock), `test_page_extraction.py::test_a_cancel_while_a_kept_page_for_extraction_is_recorded_ends_without_a_store_error`; the 202 names the runs whose cancellation it asked for (`cancellation_requested`): the run alone, `tests/integration/test_durable_runs.py::test_a_run_cancelled_while_queued_never_calls_the_model_or_reserves_spend`, its started runs alone, `test_a_finished_acquisition_s_cancel_cancels_only_its_unsettled_extractions`, or both, `test_cancelling_an_acquisition_cancels_its_queued_extractions_before_any_request`; Task 7d (intermediate review, point 1; ADR 0009 decision 15, lock order): a cancel records its requests, whose foreign keys take a key share on each run's row, before it locks the workspace's row, so a cancel concurrent with the worker writing a run's event ends without a store error on either side (two connections: the worker's transaction holds the run's row until the cancel waits for it, then writes the event; before the change PostgreSQL ended the cancel with a deadlock error, `40P01`, on every one of 10 tries), for a run and for an acquisition's started run, `test_page_extraction.py::test_a_cancel_while_the_worker_writes_a_run_s_event_ends_without_a_store_error`; fix round 1 (review Minor 1): a run that settles while the cancel waits for its row is read again once the cancel holds the workspace's row, so it is not named, and when nothing asked for is left unsettled the answer is still 409 `RUN_FINISHED` with no request, receipt or event left (two connections: the worker's terminal publish holds the row), for a run and for a finished acquisition's last started run, `test_a_cancel_whose_runs_all_settle_while_it_waits_is_run_finished`, and with one of two started runs settling the 202 names the others only, `test_a_cancel_names_only_the_runs_still_unsettled_once_it_has_waited`. T13b part verified (Task 13b final candidate; the child process is an L0 deferral, section 7): a cancel reaches a route search under way at its next check inside the worker, which stops, ends its attempt `cancelled` and stores nothing, `tests/integration/test_route_solves.py::test_a_cancel_reaches_the_search_at_its_next_check_and_stores_nothing`; a solve cancelled while queued is never started, `::test_asking_twice_is_one_solve_and_what_a_solve_cannot_start_from_is_refused`. Task 13b fix round 1: the search reads the cancel from an event a thread of its own sets, and a failed read is never a cancel, `tests/domain/test_route_solve_runs.py::test_a_cancel_the_watch_reads_stops_the_search_at_its_next_check`, `::test_a_failed_read_is_never_a_cancel_and_the_watch_reads_again`; a request after a cancel is a new run, `tests/integration/test_route_solves.py::test_a_solve_asked_for_again_after_its_cancel_is_a_new_run`; a cancel published as the worker dies, or after a crash, settles the attempt `cancelled`, `::test_a_cancel_published_as_the_worker_dies_leaves_no_attempt_unfinished`, `::test_a_solve_cancelled_after_a_crash_leaves_no_attempt_unfinished`; a solve cancelled while it waits its turn ends `cancelled` with no attempt, `::test_a_solve_cancelled_while_it_waits_its_turn_never_runs`. Open: a model request already sent is not interrupted (it completes and is booked) |

## 5 Specification section 20 non-deferrable checks

| Check | M0 pass condition | Covered by |
|---|---|---|
| Readiness/outcomes | Inspectable OUT-1 spans and rubrics; planned ≠ demonstrated; statutory gates cannot be demoted | OUT1-01–07, RDY1-01–04, MAP1-05, READINESS-01–04 |
| Money/time | TRACE-01 and FIN-1 arithmetic; prefunding counterexample; workload envelope; original dates | TRACE01-01/02/07/08, FIN1-01–12, TIME1-01–06, PLN-01–08, PREFUND-01–03, WORKLOAD-01–04, TIME-01–04 |
| Search correctness | DFS agrees with oracle; F1–F3, equal points, family variants, three-dominator case | OPT1-08, PARETO-01–03, FAMILY-01/02, TRACE01-03, DIRECTAI-01 |
| Failure semantics | Limits, failed source, cancellation, crash and true empty complete archive distinct | LIMITS-01–06, STATE-02/03, OPT1-05 |
| Permissions | Uninvited and cross-workspace access fail everywhere | SEC-01, SEC-02 |
| Secrets/spend | No key in logs, history, prompts or exports; revocation stops future calls; concurrent reservation and duplicate-spend ambiguity | MODEL-05/06/08, ARC1-06, DUR-02 |
| Untrusted input | SSRF/redirect blocks, access policy, prompt injection | ACQ-01, ACQ-04, ACQ-06 |
| Research | Snippets never full descriptions; missing data and unscored roles visible; nothing invented | JOB-05, AIR1-05, AI_RANGE-02, FUNDING-01, OPTN-03 |
| Recovery | Restart without corrupting accepted records; stale writes fail; cancellation reaches children | ARC1-05, DUR-02/04/05, VERSIONS-03, LIMITS-06 |
| UI/export | List, dendrite, scatter, sliders and Gantt share selection and filters; keyboard; print/JSON/ICS agree | VIS1-07, VIS2-01–10, EXP-01, UI-06 |
| Privacy/operations | Privacy/DPIA decision, notice and consent, deletion, basic restore | H-08, SEC-06, SEC-07, DUR-06 |

### Human review sessions (plan §10, v1.5; ruling R10; ADR 0008)

From plan v1.5 the sessions are 1, 2 and W1 (held), session W2 (a short first look) and the
end-to-end session, which replaces session 3. Each is a dispatch precondition for the work
after it. The controller does not dispatch a task whose precondition session has not
happened; it records "blocked: awaiting session N". Only the product owner's own record of a
session establishes its outcome; an agent may transcribe it, never supply it. The product
owner may rule that an AI's check of rote items counts as their own review; an agent never
assumes it. Task 16 waits for sessions 1, 2, W1, W2, the school-leaver pack session (plan
v1.6) and the end-to-end session, and every other remaining task.

| Session | When | Closes | Dispatch precondition for | Status |
